### Blocked on him — the whole list, four questions

   TF_VAR replaces the EC2 box (downtime + a Caddy cert re-issue); SSM on the
   running container survives deploys but not a box rebuild. His call, not ours.

3. **34.4 Reserved Instances** — buying the All-Upfront RDS RI at $106 with the

   −$3.27/mo EC2 (−$4.39 on Graviton).
4. **42.1 "voice is not picking"** — which browser, which device, and does it

## 0 · SERIOUS — purchasing basket (staff are blocked)

> "we got a serious bug we need to solve this on priority — staff are getting
> affected... you can notice that basket is missing in 2nd pic. if i move cursor
> that basket is flickering and hidden. somehow i tried and clicked basket, now
> when i click submit indent nothing is happening. please use playwright to test
> the functionality. this is real bug."

Two separate faults, both mine, both found in the code before touching the site.

- [x] **0.1 "Submit indent" does nothing.**
      `SheetPopup` renders through `createPortal(ui, document.body)`. A portal
      keeps the REACT tree but not the DOM tree, and `<button type="submit">`
      submits the form it is a DOM descendant of. The button is passed down
      `<form>` → `OrderFlow footer` → `BasketSheet footer` → portal, so in the
      DOM it is a child of `<body>` with no form owner at all. Clicking it is a
      no-op — exactly what he saw. Fixed with the HTML form-owner attribute
      (`<form id>` + `form="…"` on the button), which works across ancestry.
      *Broke when I extracted SheetPopup to a portal; nothing was checking it.*
- [x] **0.2 Basket disappears / flickers.**
      The drag clamps against `window.innerWidth`, which INCLUDES the vertical
      scrollbar, so the basket can be parked in the strip under it. A `fixed`
      element there overflows the page — which is the horizontal scrollbar that
      appears in his 2nd and 3rd screenshots and not the 1st. The remembered
      position was also never re-checked: it is read from localStorage and
      applied whatever the window is now. Clamp on the client width, re-clamp on
      load and on resize.
- [x] **0.3 Playwright proof** of add → basket → submit → indent created.

## 2 · Inventory

> "see here that choose (or +add supplier) word is not visible coz of yellow blur

- [x] 2.1 **Contrast**: the amber `choose · 4` chip is unreadable. (Reported
      before, for other chips — this one was missed.)
- [x] 2.2 Click `choose · N` → supplier popup **in place**, not a page change.
- [x] 2.3 `+ Add supplier` → add it **in place**; stop sending them to Vendors.
- [x] 2.4 **BUG — some rows do not open.** "i placed cursor in brinjal, i clicked,
      it's not clickable. why some item it's working, for some it's not."
- [x] 2.5 Item detail as **popup inside popup**, not the side sheet, with the
      body in sections so there is nothing to scroll. *(carried from his earlier
      message the same day)*
- [x] 2.6 More done in place — **correct the stock from the item popup**. The
      commonest reason to open an item is that the number is wrong, and fixing
      it meant a trip to Stock-take. Posts the same ADJUSTMENT movement, so the
      audit trail is unchanged; the confirmation names the delta and its value,
      because a stock correction is a money event.
- [x] 2.6b **Log waste in place** — and the reason it matters more than the
      convenience: stock going down because something spoiled is not the same
      event as stock going down because the count was wrong. Waste is a cost
      you can analyse (valued at weighted-average cost, visible on Money);
      an ADJUSTMENT is a correction with no story. Offering only "correct the
      stock" quietly pushes every spoilage through the wrong door and leaves
      the waste report empty while the money leaks. Both now sit one sentence
      apart, where the stock number is. — think of what else belongs here rather than a
      round trip.
- [x] 2.7 **MOBILE PARITY — every one of the above.**
      > "whatever we're seeing in inventory page (all the feature popups, click
      > to show etc etc) all these the exact same thing need to be in mobile view
      > too. so please don't miss in mobile by focusing only on desktop view."

      Not a narrowed-down version: the same popups, the same in-place actions,
      the same reach. Checked at 390x844, not only at 1080p.

      **He was right to say it.** The phone list is separate markup
      (`lg:hidden` cards — a six-column table on a 390px screen is not a table)
      and every fix this round went to the desktop `<tr>`. On a phone there was
      no "choose · N" chip at all, "+ add supplier" was still a `<Link>` to the
      Vendors page, and it was still in the unreadable amber. All three of the
      things he asked for, missing exactly where he predicted. Found by running
      the tests at 390px, where the rows resolved to **hidden** — both lists now
      carry one `data-testid` and the test takes whichever is visible.

- [x] 3.6 **Dashboard** — same pass. The four-line "time windows" glossary

      | /expenses | expense form | 194px | 206px |
      | /money | profit headline | 276px | 285px |
      | /reports | P&L headline | 336px | 435px |
      | /sales | takings sheet | 368px | 449px |
      | /dashboard | first KPI | 458px | 337px |

      Every core lands inside the first screen at both widths. Dashboard is the

## Evidence log

| # | What proves it | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 0.1 | Playwright on prod: add all → basket → Submit indent → `POST /purchasing/indents` observed, status < 300 | ✅ desktop + mobile |
| 0.2 | Playwright on prod: plant `{x:6000,y:4000}`, reload — basket inside the viewport, page overflow ≤ 1px, still opens | ✅ desktop + mobile |
| 2.1–2.5 | Playwright on prod: 6 rows sampled all open; chip opens a dialog with the URL unchanged; Item/Suppliers/Purchases sections each switch the body | ✅ desktop |
| 1.1–1.5 | Backend tests (scoping, range filter, totals) + Playwright driving the deployed bundle: headings render, hours total shows `96h 30m`, rostered shift appears | ✅ |
| 3.1–3.4 | Playwright on prod: core on the first screen for /sales, /expenses, /reports | ✅ |

### Proven BEFORE fixing, against the live site (2026-09-05)

Both faults reproduced on prod before a line was changed, which is the only way
to know a fix is a fix rather than a coincidence:

```
submit indent   button found, clicked, waitForRequest(POST /purchasing/indents)
                TIMED OUT after 20s — no request was ever made
basket position planted {x:6000,y:4000}; measured x+width = 6423.58
                on a 1280 viewport
```

The first version of the submit test keyed on a `data-testid` that this same
commit adds, so it could only ever report "element not found" — it could not
tell the bug from the fix not being deployed. Matching the visible words instead
is what produced the evidence above.

## 5 · Logins, email and verification

- [x] 5.1 **Super admin can change a staff member's email — this already
      exists and works.** `POST /employees/{id}/login/email` →
      `change_staff_email`, audited as `staff.email_changed`, and it is the
      "Change email (re-verification required)" field visible in his own
      screenshot, which even shows the confirmation "Email changed —
      verification sent". Because it edits the SAME user row rather than making
      a new one, everything attached to the old address follows it, which is
      exactly what he wanted. Nothing to build; worth pointing at.
- [x] 5.2 **Loosen verification.** Let them in unverified; keep the account
      usable but **pause** password-reset and alerts until the address is
      confirmed, so an unverified address cannot quietly become a recovery
      route. *Only* exception: **new-hotel registration still verifies on the
      spot** — "else suppose they give wrong mail id and we didn't verify, it
      will create so many real confusion."
- [x] 5.3 The paused things SAY so — a one-line banner on every page naming the
      two that are off. That clause of his was the important one: loosening the
      gate silently just moves the confusion to the day someone actually needs
      "forgot password" and it does nothing.

      How it is built: a `verify_required` flag set at hotel registration and
      nowhere else, so the wall stands for exactly one case. Alerts are paused
      in `notify.wants()` — the single gate every alert route passes through —
      because an unverified address is one nobody has proved they own, and a
      typo would send that hotel's payroll notices to a stranger. Transactional
      mail still goes, since that is how the address gets proved.

## 6 · The hotel's own subdomain

- [x] 6.1 On `<hotel>.dineai.cloud`, show **login only** — no Register.
      Worth recording how this nearly got ticked wrongly: `AuthGate` already
      had a `lockedToLogin` flag and already refused to switch, so reading the
      code said "done". Testing the live subdomain said otherwise — BOTH
      invitations to register were still on screen, and pressing either did
      nothing because the switch was silently refused. Dead controls read as
      broken rather than deliberate. `e2e/subdomain-login.spec.ts` now covers
      the door and the direct `/signup` URL.
- [x] 6.2 **A customisable login page per hotel.** `hotels.login_page` (JSON,
      empty = the standard door, so a hotel that never opens the panel is
      untouched), a `HotelDoor` component, and a Settings panel with a live
      preview beside the landing one.

      What can be customised: greeting and subline, three layouts (split /
      centred / card), five motion styles including **Still** — a real choice,
      not just an accessibility fallback, because a wall tablet in a kitchen
      should be allowed to stop moving — eight palettes, three moods, six
      lighting washes, logo on/off, square or soft corners, and a footer line.

      Two deliberate limits. **The form is never customised**: it is the same
      audited component on every door, because styling must not reach the thing
      that handles a password. And the mood is carried by *light* rather than a
      hero photograph — this page is on the critical path of every shift, and a
      big image is the slowest thing to put in front of someone who just wants
      to clock in. When a hotel's door is on, none of the shared DineAI cinema
      mounts at all.
- [x] 6.3 **More customisation for the hotel's landing page** — booking link
      (with its own button label), directions, Instagram, Facebook and
      WhatsApp. Chosen from what a restaurant is actually asked for rather than
      adding controls for their own sake; every one is optional and renders
      nothing when blank, so a page that predates this looks exactly as it did.

      Directions is a LINK, not an embedded map: every phone already has a maps
      app that does it better, and a third-party iframe costs this page more
      than it gives.

## 8 · Employees & Roles — the missing verbs

> "here why there is no suspend and permanently remove user feature? please
> add. but in roles page i can see that remove option suspend option in the name
> of 'X and stop access'. better chain them both (have this in both place). if
> we remove from employee then role's page need to catch that, but if we remove
> from staff, employee don't catch. keep in mind."

- [x] 8.1 **Suspend** and **permanently remove** on the Employees page. The
      confirmation for removal READS OUT what it will destroy — attendance,
      payslips, documents, shifts — fetched first from a new `/impact`
      endpoint, because every foreign key to an employee cascades and a
      one-word button should not quietly take a year of payroll with it.
- [x] 8.2 Chained: "Stop access" / "Restore access" now reads the same on both
      pages — the Employees sheet called the identical action "Deactivate this
      login", so one feature looked like two.
- [x] 8.3 **The asymmetry is deliberate and must be respected:** removing the
      EMPLOYEE removes their login too; removing their LOGIN leaves the employee
      record alone. A person can exist without an account; an account cannot
      exist without a person.
- [x] 8.4 A one-way door caught before it shipped: suspending sets
      `is_active=false`, and the roster only ever fetched ACTIVE employees — so
      a suspended person vanished from the one screen that could bring them
      back. `?include_suspended=true`, a "Show suspended" toggle, and a chip on
      the card so a parked person is never mistaken for a working one.
- [x] 8.5 Employees page UI. What was actually wrong, rather than a repaint:
      a two-row definition list for two facts, so anyone without a visa got a
      labelled row containing a dash — a column of dashes down the whole page
      on a team where most people need none; Pay, the only figure on the card,
      set at 11px like a caption; and two actions in the same weight and the
      same grey, so "open their login" and "edit their details" read as one
      control cut in half. Pay is now the figure it is, the visa appears only
      when there is one, and Login & access is the primary of the two because
      everything about the person as an ACCOUNT lives behind it.

## 10 · AI is a grant, not a default

> "also i can see ai feature is enabled coming by default for staff login, why?
> please check. only superadmin allowed to give ai feature, then only it need to
> show nah."

- [x] 10.1 **BUG, found exactly as he described.** The assistant was mounted on
      `featureOn(hotel, "ai_copilot")` alone — the HOTEL's flag — so every
      login in an AI-enabled hotel got it, including STAFF. The `ai:use`
      permission already existed and STAFF has never been granted it; the
      frontend simply never asked. Both gates now.
- [x] 10.2 **A new AI section in Roles & Access**, because there is nowhere to
      grant or withhold it today:
      > "here we don't have a feature to add or remove ai feature (under this we
      > need to have some filter like whether to give haiku or sonnet, also
      > whether to give our voice model, also what the max token max msg etc).
      > please add this feature also for role section in superadmin login."
      - on/off per person — **already a permission** (`ai:use`) and already a
        switch in the same grid, so it deliberately did NOT get a second home:
        two places to turn one thing on is how the two drift apart
      - **which model** — Haiku or Sonnet
      - **voice** on/off
      - **max tokens** and **max messages** — the ceiling, validated server-side
        rather than stored as sent, because these cap the only surface in the
        product whose cost has no natural upper bound and a typo here is a bill

      Stored on `users.ai_settings`; blank means the hotel's default, so nobody
      is capped by surprise.

> persistent. also this need to show in all lower logins too like manager staff
> etc."

- [x] 13.2 Set by whoever can configure the hotel, read by every login —

## 14 · Two things that make it feel bad to use

> "i want to click 2 or 3 time one particular area to make it select. in all the
> area im facing this issue. with seamless one click in cursor or 1 click in
> mobile i cant able to touch. i faced severely in mobile view."

- [x] 14.1 **Clicks not registering first time — TWO causes, both real, and
      worse than he described.** On a 390px screen the Sign in button produced
      no request at all: staff could not sign in on a phone.

      Found from a SCREENSHOT after two rounds of inference went nowhere. The
      picture showed a native "Please fill out this field" bubble over an EMPTY
      password, above an email that had snapped back to its default.

      1. **The page replaced itself under his finger.** The standard DineAI
         login painted immediately, then the hotel's own door arrived from the
         network and swapped it out — taking anything already typed with it and
         putting a different element under a finger that was already moving.
         Type, tap, nothing; tap again and it works. Exactly "2 or 3 times".
         A hotel subdomain now decides which door it is BEFORE painting
         anything that accepts typing.
      2. **Duplicate element ids.** The desktop cinema and the mobile card both
         mount a LoginForm at all times, both rendering `id="li-email"` — and
         `htmlFor` resolves to the FIRST match in the document, which on a
         phone is the HIDDEN desktop field. Tapping the label focused an
         invisible input: keyboard opens, caret nowhere, typing lands in a form
         nobody can see. `useId` per instance now. *(This exact trap —"the
         double #li-email" — was already in my notes from an earlier session.)*

> "i can note loading pages often. when i move from purchase page to inventory
> page i can see loading icon, which means our site is slow in showing and
> processing. please make our site fast faster."

- [x] 14.2 **Navigation is slow** — measured, then fixed where the time
      actually was. First measurement said "61 seconds", which was my own
      instrument timing out on a hidden element; a number that large should
      have been the tell, and it was. With that fixed:

      | page | first paint | API calls |
      |---|---|---|
      | /expenses | 488ms | 9 |
      | /inventory | 558ms | 7 |
      | /purchasing | 1627ms | 11 |
      | **/sales** | **6227ms** | **16** |

      /sales was the outlier and the reason was legible: **eight** separate
      `/sales/days/{date}` requests at ~600ms each — one per day of the trailing
      week, with today's fetched twice — each returning a whole day summary
      (lines, totals, cash drawer, petty cash) so the page could read ONE number
      off it for a sparkline. Replaced with `GET /sales/channel-trend`, which
      answers the whole week in one query.

      Deliberately no `response_model` on that endpoint: the payload is keyed by
      whatever the hotel names its channels, which no fixed schema describes —
      and response_model silently drops undeclared fields, a trap this project
      has been caught by four times.

## 18 · The clock popup

> "i can see scroll bar here, why the hell. i hate scroll. please fit to screen
> or make card bit bigger to ignore the scroll bar. also that 24h 12h toggle not
> working, i tried to click but its not working. same like that change
> restaurant timezone also not working. same for that face selection feature too"

- [x] 18.1 It scrolls. Fit it — the dial sits BESIDE its controls now on a wider card, two short columns instead of one tall one.
- [x] 18.2 **NOT my regression — a landmine I merely tripped.** Nothing inside the popup responds — the 12/24
      toggle, the timezone link and the face picker were all dead. Measured
      against the live API rather than assumed:

      ```
      PATCH /hotels/me {"prefs":{"clock_12h":true}}
      422  {"detail":"Unknown preference(s): kds_code"}
      ```

      `app/ordering/router.py` GENERATES and stores `prefs["kds_code"]` the
      first time anyone opens the kitchen screen, and `prefs.py` never declared
      it — while the guard validated the whole MERGED bag rather than the
      incoming change. So from the moment that code shipped, **every**
      preference write in the product failed with 422 for any hotel that had
      used the kitchen screen. Not just the clock: all of them.

      Fixed at the cause, twice over: `kds_code` is declared, and validation now
      only judges what the request is actually changing — a key another module
      stored can never again brick unrelated writes. My clock code was also
      swallowing the error, which is exactly why a 422 looked like a dead
      button; failures are shown now.

      than up to thirty seconds later.

### Two logins that were in the staff room and should not have been

Both found by reading the group picker in a screenshot, not by testing.

**The platform operator.** Support accounts are attached to a hotel so they can
help it, which also placed them inside Everyone — reading a conversation nobody
in the restaurant knew they were in. Access to run a hotel is not consent to sit
in its break room.

**The kiosk.** The attendance tablet is not a person. It sits unattended on a
counter where anyone can touch it, which is exactly why it already reaches
nothing that reveals what people earn. A private staff conversation left open on
that screen is the same exposure by another route, and worse, because chat is
where people speak freely.

Both are now outside the standing rooms and off the list of people to add, each
pinned by its own test.

### The "scroll bar" on the door — found by looking, 2026-09-06

    "also see this: i can see scroll bar here. why the hell. i hate scroll"

It was never a scrollbar. `html { scrollbar-gutter: stable }` reserves ten
pixels on the right for all time, so no page jumps sideways when it grows tall
enough to scroll. On every ordinary page that is invisible, because html's
background IS the page's background. The door is the one page that paints a
palette of its own — the hotel's, not the product's — so the reserved gutter
stayed the app's near-black and showed as a dark seam down the edge of his
restaurant's front door.

A sign-in screen is one screen and never scrolls, so it had nothing to gain from
the reservation and a visible seam to lose by it. The door now releases the
gutter while it is on screen and takes the full width.

Worth noting how it was found: the automated check passed. It measured
`scrollHeight - clientHeight = 0` and called the page clean, and it was right —
the page genuinely does not scroll. The seam was only ever visible in the
screenshot.

### Verified on the live site, 2026-09-06

Run against `https://nirai1.dineai.cloud` on the deployed build, signed in as the
real superadmin. Not "the code looks right" — the buttons were pressed.

**Team chat** — `e2e/team-chat.spec.ts`, 4/4 passed:
- Everyone and Managers both listed and named.
- A group created through the UI, a message sent with an emoji from the picker,
  the message still there after a full page reload, then the group closed —
  so nothing was left behind in anybody's room.
- On a 390px phone: the list is the screen, tapping a room replaces it, Back
  returns, and the composer is in the viewport without scrolling.

**Live API probe** — 15/15 passed, including the refusals: an empty message is
400, Everyone refuses a hand-edited member list, and Everyone cannot be closed.
The probe group was deleted afterwards and the tenant re-checked: only the two
standing rooms remain.

**Batches 5–7** — `e2e/batch-0906.spec.ts`, 4/4 passed:
- The 12/24 clock survives a **reload**, which is the assertion that matters —
  it moved before and forgot, because a 422 was being swallowed. Put back as
  found afterwards.
- /plan asks one question at a time; switching tabs genuinely changes the page.
- /audit and /documents both put real content on the first screen.
- /my says something true. The owner's login is not linked to an employee
  record, so it explains that and names the fix rather than showing a blank —
  and the owner's end of the staff thread answers 200.

Two of those four "failures" on the first run were **my test, not the product**:
the plan tabs carry an explicit `role="tab"` which overrides the implicit button
role, and I had demanded a staff composer from an owner account. The screenshots
settled both — the pages had been correct the whole time.

Two real bugs the screenshots DID find, now fixed:
- The chat panes filled only a third of a laptop screen. `lg:h-full` resolved to
  auto (main's height is indefinite to a percentage child) and the grid's
  implicit row was sized to its content. Measured at 365px inside a 735px main.
- The composer placeholder wrapped and clipped mid-word on a phone.

- [x] 21.8 Nine-test suite: role visibility, the 404-not-403 leak, persistence,
      unread arithmetic, group membership editing, standing rooms being
      un-closable, attachment type/scoping, and cross-hotel isolation.


## 22 · Rota and Attendance, rebuilt from scratch (+ Menu)

> "add ui enhancement in menu, rota attendance — mainly rota and attendance bro.
>  UI UX we need to change from the scratch, we need to build the super cool
>  UI UX. try think deeply and work on"

**Measured on the live site before touching anything.** Screens tall, counted
against the viewport:

| page | desktop | phone | what is on screen when you land |
|---|---|---|---|
| /rota | 1.39 | 1.94 | leave records, six export buttons, a five-field form |
| /attendance | 1.97 | **4.41** | a cartoon chef, a history panel, kiosk setup steps |
| /menu | 1.85 | 4.27 | 47 input fields, 77 buttons |

The diagnosis is the same on both of the two that matter, and it is not a
styling problem. **The page buries the thing it is for.**

On Rota the week grid — the entire point — begins about 900px down, under a
tab strip that only scrolls, a permanently-open Leave panel, six export buttons
and an inline "Add a shift" form. Saturday and Sunday are clipped off the right.

On Attendance the six people begin about 1000px down, under a history panel, a
punch clock illustrated with a 180px cartoon chef, and a numbered how-to for
setting up the door tablet. A legend and a flow explainer sit below them. On a
phone you reach the end of the first screen without seeing a single person.

Both pages are laid out as a manual with the tool at the bottom.

- [x] 22.1 **Attendance: the people first.** One sticky row — day stepper, who
      is in, and a ⋯ for everything else — then the staff as cards. The punch
      clock stops being a section and becomes what the card DOES: one primary
      action that changes with state (Clock in → Break → Clock out).
- [x] 22.2 **Attendance: tap a person → popup.** The day's punches, back-dating
      a missed punch, booking leave, and that person's history with totals —
      all the things that were three separate panels.
- [x] 22.3 **Attendance: the table goes.** A `<table>` of seven columns is why
      the phone reads 4.41 screens. Cards work at both sizes.
- [x] 22.4 **Rota: the week first.** Sticky week stepper with the labour figure,
      then the grid immediately, fitting seven days without clipping.
- [x] 22.5 **Rota: add a shift where the shift goes.** Tap a day → popup already
      knowing which day. Better than a form with a day dropdown, and it removes
      the form from the page.
- [x] 22.6 **Rota: leave, exports, copy-week and the legend into ⋯.** Occasional
      jobs should not outrank the week.
- [x] 22.7 **Menu: dishes as tiles, editing in a popup** — 47 inline inputs is a
      wall to scroll, not a menu to read.
- [x] 22.8 Mobile parity, as always: the same features, not a reduced set.
- [x] 22.9 Verified on the live site with screenshots I actually looked at:
      rota (1.39 → 1.0 screens, all seven days, nothing clipped), attendance
      (4.41 → the whole page in one screen, the table gone), menu (47 inline
      inputs → tiles with one toggle each), the chat at full height, the door
      with its seam gone, and the page studio.

      Two things the pictures caught that the assertions could not. The chat
      panes filled a third of a laptop screen while every check passed. And my
      own fix for the cramped studio controls — collapsing every inner grid to
      one column — turned the hero-photo picker into six full-width photographs
      stacked down the page, again with every number green. Grids were never
      the problem; width was.

      One artifact worth naming so it is not "fixed" later: in a fullPage
      capture the mobile nav bar appears in the MIDDLE of the page. Fixed
      elements photograph at their viewport position. Nothing is wrong.

## 23 · One place for every conversation

> "here itself integrate all message related for super admin — like hotel to
>  hotel in one side, within hotel one on one or grp or anything is other side.
>  but we need a best whatsapp style UI UX with emoji gif video audio image doc
>  etc, anything we can send via chat."
> "one to one within the hotel — like any staff can chat with anyone, like
>  organisation in teams"
> "seriously worst place to keep" — on finding the 1:1 thread at the bottom of
>  an employee's record.

**What was actually wrong, checked across every role before building.**

Talking to another restaurant lived on /messages. Talking to your team lived on
/chat. Talking to ONE colleague lived at the bottom of their record on the
Employees admin page. Three screens, three shapes, one activity — and the third
one had a consequence nobody would guess from looking at it:

| role | Everyone | Managers | make groups | one-to-one |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Super admin | yes | yes | yes | buried in Employees |
| Manager | yes | yes | no | buried in Employees |
| Kitchen manager | yes | yes | no | **none at all** |
| Accountant | yes | no | no | buried in Employees |
| Cashier | yes | no | no | **none at all** |
| Staff | yes | no | no | My Space → Messages |
| Kiosk tablet | no | no | no | no (deliberate) |

A chef and a cashier could not message anybody, because the only door to a 1:1
was a page that needs `employees:read` to open.

- [x] 23.1 **One Messages page.** Two sides: this hotel, and the other
      restaurants. Same composer, same bubbles, same unread counts — a message
      is a message. /messages forwards, so old links still land somewhere.
- [x] 23.2 **One-to-one with anyone.** A direct conversation is now a ROOM
      between two users rather than a thread hanging off an employee record, so
      it inherits attachments, emoji and unread counts for free. Any login can
      start one.
- [x] 23.3 **`/chat/people` carries no permission.** It used to need
      `users:read` — the permission for administering logins — which is why only
      an owner or manager could start a conversation. A porter knowing a chef
      works here is not a disclosure; they share a kitchen. Email is still held
      back unless you administer logins: a name and a job is what you need to
      pick somebody from a list, an address book is a different thing to hand out.
- [x] 23.4 **A→B and B→A are one conversation.** `dm_key` is the two ids sorted,
      with a unique index, so two people opening each other in the same second
      cannot make two rooms that each hold half the history.
- [x] 23.5 **Emoji, photo, video, audio and documents** in every conversation.
      Documents were refused before, on the reasoning that chat should not
      become a filing cabinet; he is right that a rota PDF is a normal thing to
      hand a colleague. Executables are still refused.
- [x] 23.6 **The old thread is carried across**, not stranded on a page nobody
      will visit again. The migration copies it into the direct room and leaves
      the original table untouched, so a mistake costs nothing.
- [x] 23.7 An empty conversation does not appear in anybody's list — opening the
      picker and changing your mind should not leave a room behind for ever.

- [x] 23.8 **A real emoji picker.** The first cut was thirty characters I had
      chosen, which is a shortcut bar and not a picker: the one you want is the
      one that is not there, and there is no way to look for it. Now ~300 across
      six categories, a search box that crosses all of them, and your recent
      ones kept at the top. No library — emoji are Unicode, so a picker is a
      list and a filter, and a 400KB dependency to render text would be paying a
      download for what the font already does.
- [ ] 23.9 ⛔ BLOCKED — **GIF search**, through our own server so the key is never published
      in front-end JavaScript. A chosen GIF is FETCHED and stored like any other
      attachment rather than hot-linked: the joke somebody sent in March should
      still be there in June, not depending on somebody else's CDN. Only Tenor's
      own host is fetched, because taking a URL from a client and asking the
      server to go and get it is how a server gets used to reach places a
      browser could not.
      **NEEDS A KEY FROM HIM** — a free Tenor key, set as `TENOR_API_KEY`.
      Without it the picker says so plainly instead of showing an empty grid,
      and sending a .gif FILE keeps working either way.
      ⚠️ **HOW it gets set is his call, and I have not chosen for him.** The
      other keys travel GitHub secret → TF_VAR → `user_data.sh.tftpl`, and
      `user_data_replace_on_change = true` — so adding one there REPLACES THE
      EC2 BOX: downtime, a fresh instance and a Caddy cert re-issue. I have
      already taken this site down once this project and will not do that
      unasked. The safe alternative is setting it on the running container via
      SSM, which survives normal deploys (images roll in place) but not a box
      rebuild. The backend reads the variable either way.

## 24 · The AI toggles were on the wrong sheet — and nothing read them

> "i said u to add ai related role toggles too nah. u said u added..but where?
>  i can see here" — looking at the per-JOB sheet on Roles & Access.

He was right twice over.

**They were on the wrong sheet.** The panel existed, on the per-PERSON card.
That is the exception, not the rule: "so manager means what and all he can
access, super admin can choose this" is a sentence about a JOB. Setting the
model once per role is a setting; setting it on every person who ever holds that
role is a chore.

**And nothing obeyed them.** `ai_settings` was stored on the user, returned by
the API and rendered in the UI — and no code path in the assistant ever read it.
Storage nothing reads is not a setting, it is a note. The model, the voice and
the caps were decorative.

- [x] 24.1 `role_defaults.ai_settings` — what the AI may do for everyone with
      this job, with the panel on the sheet he was actually looking at.
- [x] 24.2 A person's own settings still win over their job's, exactly as their
      permissions do.
- [x] 24.3 `guard.model_for` now READS them. The plan is still the ceiling: a
      cheaper model always applies, a dearer one cannot exceed what the hotel
      bought — a setting must never be a way around the paywall.
- [x] 24.4 One validator for both, so the per-person and per-job paths cannot
      drift — and the way they would drift is that one stops enforcing a cap,
      which is the entire point of them.

## 25 · The batch of 2026-09-07

- [x] 25.1 **Realtime chat.** "its not realtime, i can see small delay" — a
      six-second poll makes every message three seconds old on average. Chat now
      rides the Server-Sent Events stream the hotel already keeps open for live
      PO updates. The event carries NO text, only "something changed in room X":
      everyone in the hotel is on that stream, including people who cannot open
      the room, so putting the message on the wire would hand a private
      conversation to the whole building.
- [x] 25.2 **The chat felt tight, and it was not the padding.** Every message
      repeated the sender's name and the full date. A conversation reads as one
      because it DROPS what has not changed: a run from one person shows one
      name, one avatar and one time, and the day appears once where it changes.
- [x] 25.3 **Team chat + Messages merged** into one page, both sides intact —
      shipped in 18daf4a, live.
- [x] 25.4 **A bare ⋯ is a secret.** "user will think there is no extra
      features" — and everything behind it still worked, so nothing looked
      broken; the exports and the tablet setup simply ceased to exist for anyone
      who never guessed. It says "More" in words now, with a count.
- [x] 25.5 **Attendance shows the figures on the card** — in, out, break, hours.
      They were the old table's columns and they were right to be visible:
      they are what you scan.
- [x] 25.6 **Messaging left the personnel record.** The employee card links to
      Messages instead of carrying its own thread — two doors into one
      conversation is how two conversations start.
- [x] 25.7 **Comments on a document request**, both sides, persistent, live.
      Not chat messages: "this photo is blurred, send another" belongs beside
      the photo, and six months later the reason a document was re-requested
      three times has to sit with the document.
- [x] 25.8 **The clock buttons** land instantly instead of waiting on a round
      trip while disabled, and the **timezone link** now waits for the content
      before scrolling — the anchor was always right, the page just had not
      rendered it yet.

## 26 · AI under AI, and a popup you can read

- [x] 26.1 **The AI panel sits INSIDE the assistant card**, with the switch that
      turns it on. It was a separate block at the top of the sheet, so the thing
      that grants AI and the settings that shape it were in two different places
      on one screen. Repeating the switch inside the panel would have been the
      other fix, and the wrong one — two places to turn one thing on is how the
      two drift apart.
- [x] 26.2 **Custom roles get it too.** `custom_roles.ai_settings`, and
      `effective_ai` resolving three layers narrowest-last: built-in job, then
      the role the hotel invented, then the person.
- [x] 26.3 `RoleOut` declares `ai_settings`. It did not, and response_model
      drops what it is not told about — the SIXTH time this project has lost a
      field that saved correctly and vanished on the way to the browser.
- [x] 26.4 **The scrollbars inside the role card** are gone and the sheet is
      taller. The modal's own comment claims neither pane ever scrolls; that
      stopped being true once the panes grew.
- [x] 26.5 **The popup text was not blurred — the contrast was too low.** The

## 28 · Speed, voice, and one duplicate

### The site feels heavy

> "site got heavy so site is always loading even for small small movement and
>  navigations... i need our site to feel smooth, instant loading, that i should
>  not see that loading animation itself, that kinda latency we need."
> "also i need to click one any button 2 or 3 times to make it click."

- [x] 28.1 **Measured, and the answer was not the server.** Per page: painted
      517–950ms, spinner gone 708–2077ms, **8–16 API calls**, payloads 17–82KB,
      slowest call ~640ms of which ~635ms was WAITING.
      Then the decisive one: `/api/health` does no database work and still takes
      ~300ms on a warm connection, while a bare TCP connect takes 284ms. That
      300ms is the round trip from his desk to London. **A bigger instance
      cannot move the speed of light**, and payload size rules out bandwidth.
      Fifteen calls at six-at-a-time is three waves — about a second of waiting
      before the server has done anything.
- [x] 28.2 **Kill the spinner on navigation.** A page that already has its data
      should not blank and re-fetch to show the same thing.
- [x] 28.3 **The two- and three-tap problem**, again. It was fixed once for the
      shell's smallest targets; this is something else.
- [x] 28.4 **Infrastructure was NOT the answer, and I have not touched it.**
      The measurements say the cost is round trips, not compute. Spending money
      on a bigger box would have left him with the same wait and a larger bill.
      If a future measurement shows server think time dominating, that is when
      the box matters.

### Voice

> "only listening the 1st sentence and started give answer. if i say 2nd
>  sentence its not listening, its talking to itself... only when i turn off and
>  turn on then only its listening, that too for first time."

- [x] 28.5 **It stops listening after one turn.** A conversation is not one
      question, and the fix is not "press the button again".
- [x] 28.6 **Actions should not open the bubble and slam it shut.** "for
      navigation action and all no need to open and auto close the bubble chat,
      its irritating." Open it only when there is something to SHOW — a table,
      a chart — because that is what the bubble is for.
- [x] 28.7 **The circle-inside-circle centre animation does not fit its card.**
      SHIPPED. Measured on live rather than guessed at, after three earlier
      guesses missed it. On the stage: orb 72px, ring box 86px (they sit at
      `inset: -10%`), card 94px tall — and each ring SCALES with the voice,
      `1 + level * 0.05 * r`, so the outermost ring reaches 86 × 1.2 = 103px
      into a 94px box with `overflow: clip`. The outer ring lost its edge
      exactly when the voice was loudest, which is precisely when you are
      looking at it. Padding the wrapper was the earlier attempt and could
      never have worked: the clip happens at the CARD, and the wrapper is
      outside it.
      The fix is to notice that on the stage this card has no background, no
      border, no shadow and no backdrop-filter — it is not a surface, so it has
      nothing to contain. `clip` was doing no visual work and charging a
      clipped ring for it. Scoped to the stage only: inside the panel the card
      IS a surface, and there `clip` stays, because it is what stops the
      contents shunting sideways.

### Small

- [x] 28.8 **"The assistant · The assistant"** — the AI row lists its two pages
      and I gave them the same label when merging them. One assistant, one chip.

## 29 · Speed: measured, decided, and what we are NOT buying

> "we gonna create another server instance or ecs moving thing? whats ur finding
>  and suggestion"
> — and his decision: "no need to build that south replication, its extra cost
>  unwanted."

**The finding.** `/api/health` does no database work and still takes ~300ms on a
warm connection, while a bare TCP connect takes 284ms. That is the round trip
from his desk to London. Payloads are 17–82KB, so not bandwidth. The server is
not short of anything — **a bigger instance, more instances or ECS would have
cost money and moved nothing.**

**The click delay was a different problem entirely**, and not the network at all.
A press state paints without asking a server anything, so a late one means the
main thread was busy:

| page | long tasks | worst | blocked | DOM nodes | animations |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| /dashboard | 1 | 87ms | 37ms | 649 | 12 |
| /inventory | 6 | **831ms** | **1738ms** | **3591** | 23 |
| /sales | 1 | 58ms | 8ms | 322 | 11 |

- [x] 29.1 Fewer round trips: in-flight GETs deduplicated, a 3s read cache, and
      longer windows for the five near-static shell calls every page was paying
      for. Attendance's seven-day bars went from SEVEN requests to one.
- [x] 29.2 Main thread: nothing past the twelfth row animates, and long tables
      use `content-visibility` so off-screen rows cost nothing.
- [x] 29.3 A moving target is a missed tap: the staggered entry animation
      displaced every row 12px for up to a second, and re-renders restarted it.
      It fades now.
- [x] 29.4 **DECIDED: no Mumbai region, no bigger box.** The only change that
      would meaningfully cut the remaining ~300ms is serving from ap-south-1,
      and he has judged the cost not worth it. Recorded here so nobody
      re-opens it as though it were an oversight.

### Coverage was one hair from breaking the build

- [x] 29.5 The last deploy passed the gate at **70.01%** against a 70%
      threshold — about two uncovered statements from failing. Two features I
      shipped without tests were part of it: document comments, and the GIF
      endpoints. Both are now tested for the things that actually matter — who
      can read a thread, and that the server will only fetch from one host.
- [x] 29.6 **But eleven new tests moved the total by 0.04pp — six statements —
      and that turned out to be the real story.** Coverage was not measuring
      most of what the suite runs.
      SQLAlchemy's async layer does its synchronous work inside a GREENLET.
      Without `concurrency` set, coverage.py loses its trace function across a
      greenlet switch, so in every endpoint that touches the database the first
      `await` into SQLAlchemy is recorded and **every line after it in that
      function is reported missing**.
      The proof it was measurement rather than dead code: a test asserts a 400
      that can only be raised at `teamchat/router.py:231`, it passes, and
      coverage called that line unexecuted.
      So the gate was never tight because the suite was thin. It was tight
      because most of what the suite exercises was invisible to the counter.
      `concurrency = ["greenlet", "thread"]` is declared now, and the result
      settles it:

      | | before | after |
      |---|---|---|
      | total coverage | 70.05% | **83.22%** |
      | statements missed | 4432 | **2483** |
      | app/documents/comments.py | 62% | **100%** |
      | app/teamchat/router.py | 54% | **78%** |

      **1,949 statements were being exercised and not counted.** The suite was
      never at 70% — it was at 83% and the counter could not see across a
      greenlet switch. Every hour anybody has spent writing tests to nurse that
      gate was spent on a measurement fault.

      Worth deciding separately: a 70% gate with real coverage at 83% is 13
      points of slack and protects very little. Raising it to ~80% would make it
      mean something again. Not done unilaterally — it changes when CI says no.

### What the speed work actually bought — measured before and after

**Main thread** (this is what made a press look late):

| page | blocked before | blocked after | worst task |
|---|---|---|---|
| /inventory | 1738ms | **139ms** | 831ms → 120ms |
| /sales | 8ms | **0ms** | 58ms → 0 |
| /dashboard | 37ms | 34ms | already fine |

**Navigation**, measured by CLICKING the sidebar rather than reloading — the
first measurement used `page.goto()`, which is a full browser reload and throws
away the in-memory cache, so it measured the one case the cache cannot help and
reported that the cache had done nothing:

| going to | calls before | calls after |
|---|---|---|
| Inventory | 8 | **3** |
| Sales & Cash | 11 | **6** |
| Rota | 12 | **4** |

The five shell calls every page used to repeat — who am I, announcements,
onboarding, unread counts — are gone entirely between navigations. What remains
is the page's own data, which has to be fetched. No spinner was observed at any
point in the walk.

First paint on a cold load also roughly halved (dashboard 771→392ms, inventory
950→243ms).

### Rota

- [x] 30.1 **BUG — the same shift can be added twice.** He pressed Add twice for
      Mohamed and got two identical shifts, same person, same day, same times.
      The server accepted both.
- [x] 30.2 **BUG, small and serious — "today" is on the wrong day.** The week
      shows Mon 7 Sept, his laptop says 7 Sept, and the chip sits on Tue 8/9.
      The marker is being decided by the BROWSER's date rather than the
      restaurant's, so anyone ahead of London sees tomorrow marked as today.
- [x] 30.3 **Copy-a-week belongs on the page**, not buried in ⋯.
- [x] 30.4 **A day with more than about five people scrolls inside its own
      card**, which is horrible to read and worse to drag into.
- [x] 30.5 **Dragging is clumsy** — the card scrolls under the drag.
      SHIPPED. Two halves. The card scrolling under the drag is gone — days
      grow downwards now instead of scrolling inside themselves. CONFIRMED ON
      LIVE, not assumed: all seven day columns measure `scrollHeight ===
      clientHeight` with `overflow-y: visible`, and a sweep of every descendant
      found zero inner scrollers.
      The other half was worse than clumsy. **HTML5 drag events do not fire on
      touch at all**, so on his phone the rota could not be rearranged — not
      awkwardly, at all. The shift editor now carries a row of seven day
      buttons: one tap to move, works on a phone, works with a keyboard, and
      undoes like any other move. Drag stays for the desktop; it is just no
      longer the only way.
- [x] 30.6 **Undo tickets stack one per move.** Move six shifts and the list
      pushes the week off the screen. Needs to collapse.
- [x] 30.7 Think of something genuinely useful to add here — his words: "any
      other creative feature that will make the user feel comfortable and
      impressed".

      SHIPPED. **The week check.** The page could already say what a week
      COSTS; it could not say whether the week is any GOOD, and those are
      different questions — a rota is wrong long before it is expensive. Three
      things it now surfaces, all computed from shifts already on the page with
      no extra fetch: a day with nobody on it and nobody on leave (usually an
      unfinished rota), anyone over 48h, and anyone working six or seven days
      straight. 48h is the UK Working Time Regulations weekly average, over 17
      weeks — so it WARNS and never blocks: a genuine long week during a
      festival is legal, and a rota tool that refuses to build one is a rota
      tool people stop using. The strip only appears when there is something to
      say, because a panel that says "all fine" every day is a panel nobody
      reads on the day it matters. Each flag carries an ⓘ explaining itself.

### The employee popup

- [x] 30.8 **Still scrolls.** Make it big enough to hold what it holds, or
      section it — "whatever, i need best UI UX".
      SHIPPED `7e7b9e5`. Measured on live first: the sheet's body held 561px

- [x] 30.9 **The header block reads raw** — the address, the chips and the

      SHIPPED `7e7b9e5`. It was a monospace email, three chips and a lowercase
      role printed on one line at four different weights, with a paragraph of
      small coloured text under it carrying a parenthesis about grandfathered
      accounts. It read as debug output because structurally that is what it
      was: four unrelated values in a row, plus a footnote. Now one identity
      card — the address as a heading, the role as its caption, and only the two
      states that change what you can do kept as chips.

### Attendance

- [x] 30.10 **"1 expected, not in" needs an ⓘ** — it is a good sentence and a
      confusing one at a glance.
      SHIPPED `7e7b9e5`. New `components/InfoDot.tsx` — a click-to-open note,
      not a `title=` tooltip: hover does not exist on the phone he checks this
      on. It says what "expected" is actually doing (on the rota today, not
      clocked in, not on booked leave) which is a narrower and more useful set
      than "everybody missing".
- [x] 30.11 **Leave opens INSIDE the sheet** and makes a scrollbar. It should be
      a section or its own popup.
      SHIPPED `7e7b9e5`. Root cause: it was an `absolute` dropdown 288px wide
      anchored to a button that sits at the bottom of a popup whose body is
      `overflow-y:auto`. An absolutely positioned child still counts towards its
      scroll container's content, so opening it grew the sheet's scroll height —
      the scrollbar he saw. It is a stacked `SheetPopup` now (depth 2), sectioned
      into what-kind / which-days / why, with an inclusive day count.
- [x] 30.12 **The pale red, everywhere.** "looking like pale color, not nice,
      which making text also blur — we need to show strongly, but dont over
      darken and make worse." Same fault as the employee popup: a light-theme
      red chosen for a dark ground.
      SHIPPED `7e7b9e5` + `02488c8`. THREE separate causes, found by measuring
      on live rather than reading the CSS:
      1. **Portals.** The light-theme rescue is written
         `.mise-app[data-mode="light"] .text-…`, and `.mise-app` is a div in the
         shell while every popup renders with `createPortal` into `<body>` — a
         sibling, not a descendant. So the rescue could never apply inside a
         popup. Measured: "1 expected, not in" on the page is rgb(190,18,60) at
         9.2:1; the same family of class inside a popup two inches away stayed
         at its pale dark-theme value. That is why "Save this day" and "Copy 3
         onto this week" were pink text on a pink fill — not blurred, just
         barely a different colour from their own background. The mode is
         stamped on `<html>` now, an ancestor of every portal there can be.
         (Two components had already hit this and hand-patched around it
         locally — three workarounds for one missing ancestor.)
      2. **The tones were muddy.** Chips mixed their hue 62% into `--color-fg`,
         which on light is a blue-black slate, so #f43f5e landed on
         rgb(157,48,74) — a mauve. It PASSES contrast, which is why no automated
         check ever complained, and it still looks washed out because the eye
         reads saturation as strength. Each tone now names its own ink at the
         700 stop of its own hue.
      3. **Forty-four classes were never on the list** — opacity modifiers
         (`text-brand-300/90` compiles to a class `.text-brand-300` cannot
         match), `text-amber-500` (11 uses, 2.1:1 on white), the `-100` shades,
         and every `hover:` variant. The block is generated from the classes
         actually in the tree now, covering 31 across five hues.
- [x] 30.13 **No way back** from the person popup.
      SHIPPED `7e7b9e5`. `SheetPopup` grew an `onBack` prop: a ← in the header,
      and Escape / Android-Back now mean the smallest step backwards rather than
      dumping you two levels down. Booking leave hands the person straight back
      instead of closing everything.
- [x] 30.14 **The popup is too small** — reaching parts of it needs scrolling.

      SHIPPED `7e7b9e5`. `columns={2}` → `columns={3}`. It carries a month of
      history — a heatmap, a bar chart and a day-by-day list — and was drawing
      them in 40rem.

### Menu

- [x] 30.15 **Rebuild the page from scratch.** The cards are not nice to look
      at; follow the UI of the pages that are.

      SHIPPED `e678e40`. Diagnosed from the screenshot, not from the code:
      thirteen dishes rendered as thirteen identical rows, each saying "Main"
      and each carrying a solid dark-red pill reading "On the menu". A word

### Tables & QR

- [x] 30.16 **Rebuild the page from scratch.**
      SHIPPED `bb36031`. Nineteen tables meant nineteen raised slabs, each with
      a 145px QR, three download buttons and its URL in monospace — 57 buttons
      and four screens of scroll to answer "which tables do I have". The QR is
      also the least interesting thing there: printed once, laminated, never
      looked at again, yet taking 80% of the space every day. Tiles now, opening
      a popup, in the house style. The printable sheet is separate and
      print-only, because a print card wants to be large, isolated and
      unclickable while a management tile wants the opposite.
- [x] 30.17 **Put the table's name in the CENTRE of its QR**, surrounded by the
      code, so a glance at the card tells you which table it is.
      SHIPPED `bb36031`. The plate is 26% of the QR's side — 6.8% of its area —
      knocked out of a symbol generated at error="h" (~30% of modules
      recoverable). VERIFIED BY DECODING, not by trusting the maths: OpenCV
      reads it at every label length tested and every scale from 164px up, and
      the real failure point sits between 38% and 44% of the side. That leaves
      ~1.5x of margin for the actual enemies — a thumbprint, a splash of curry,
      a phone camera in dim light. The centre is also the cheapest place to
      spend it: the finder squares and timing rows live at the edges.
- [x] 30.18 **Creating several tables should let each be named**, not
      "Table 1..10".
      SHIPPED `bb36031`. `BulkTablesIn.labels` — names are used in turn and
      anything past the end of the list falls back to the numbering, so naming
      three of ten costs three keystrokes rather than ten. Indexed on how many
      have been MADE rather than the loop counter, so a skipped duplicate
      cannot silently consume somebody's name and shift every table after it.
- [x] 30.19 **Rename after creation**, and regenerate the QR.
      SHIPPED `bb36031`. Rename and seats live in the table's popup. The QR
      redraws because it renders the label live — the code itself is never
      touched, since that card is sitting on a table and must keep working.
      `Cache-Control: no-cache` on the endpoint plus a cache-buster on the
      `<img>`, because a cached QR would keep printing the old name.
- [x] 30.20 Add something genuinely useful here too.

      SHIPPED `bb36031`. The third line of every tile says whether anyone is
      sitting there — order count, how long they have been seated, and a ✋ when
      they have asked for someone. Every fact needed was already in the orders
      table; nobody had joined them up. One grouped query for the whole room
      rather than one per table, derived rather than stored so it cannot drift
      out of step with the orders, and declared explicitly on `TableOut` because
      `response_model` drops undeclared fields (this project has lost six that
      way).

## 31 — sent 7 Sept, mid-session

- [x] 31.1 **BUG — a move duplicated the shift, and the duplicate could not be
      undone.** "here i moved 1 member from today to next day but it got
      duplicated... then i clicked undo last, 1 undo done, duplicate stayed here
      itself, there is no undo."
      SHIPPED `7e7b9e5`. Two faults, one symptom. The move was a CREATE on the
      new day followed by a DELETE of the old one — between those calls the
      shift really does exist twice on the server. And the undo tickets were
      keyed on a TEMPORARY id, with the server row only deleted once that id had
      been swapped for a real one; so an undo pressed before the POST returned
      restored the original and left the created row standing with nothing
      pointing at it. A duplicate no undo could reach, exactly as he described.
      A move is one fact changing, so it is one `PATCH /rota/shifts/{id}` now.
      The shift keeps its id, so undo is the same call pointing back and cannot
      arrive too early. Plus a synchronous ref rejecting a second drop inside
      one gesture, and migration `8407e01770df` — a UNIQUE index on
      (hotel, employee, date, start, end), because the endpoint's check is a
      read-then-write that two simultaneous requests can still race past. The
      migration deletes the duplicates already on his tenant first: a fix that
      only prevents new ones leaves the ones he can see.
- [x] 31.2 **Ctrl+Y to redo, as well as Ctrl+Z.** "both i want."
      SHIPPED `7e7b9e5`. Ctrl+Y (the Windows spelling, and he is on Windows) and
      Ctrl+Shift+Z. Neither fires while the focus is in a text field. A Redo
      button sits in the ticket strip beside Undo, and the strip now survives
      the undo list going empty so there is somewhere for redo to live.
- [x] 31.3 **The pale red again — "in so many pages i guess, please check and
      solve all area."** Folded into 30.12 above; see the three root causes
      there. `02488c8` is the app-wide sweep.

### Roles & Access — the flexibility problem

- [x] 32.4 **Permissions are bundled per GROUP, and he needs them per PAGE.**
      "I made the sale and cash page to be cross. Now in that 3 pages (1 is

      Each page needs its own no-access / can-see / can-change.
      SHIPPED. He is right that it was bundled, and there was a real reason:
      Sales & Cash, Online Orders and Money all read the same data, so ONE
      module permission guards all three — you cannot grant "write sales" for
      one screen and withhold it for another at the data layer, because it is
      the same data.
      What CAN be done honestly is take a screen's write actions away while
      leaving its neighbours alone. `page:<slug>:ro` only ever NARROWS: it
      cannot grant anything the module permission refused, which is what makes
      it safe for the UI to decide, and it is documented as a UI restriction in
      `rbac.py` so nobody later mistakes it for a data guarantee.
      The page chips in the role builder are three-state now — can change /
      can only look / hidden — cycling on click, because there are only three
      answers and they have a natural order.
      **The bigger half:** answering this revealed that Online Orders had NO
      write gate at all. Anyone who could open it could move a ticket, reprice
      the menu, pause the kitchen and mint a rider PIN. Making it read-onlyABLE
      required first making it gated.
      **REBUILT 2026-09-09 after he rejected the first version.** The first cut
      was a cycling chip that only offered read-only when the area was already
      on "can change" — so on an area set to "Can see", which is the state he
      screenshotted, it did nothing at all. He asked for "popup kinda thing like
      ask in these 3 page what role u want to give", so each page now gets its
      own Hidden / Can look / Can change, and the chips became a readout.
      Rule: the area is the MAXIMUM of its pages, and a page that was only
      readable before the area rose stays readable rather than being promoted
      behind him.
      Verified live, click by click: Online Orders → Can look, Sales & Cash →
      Can change, Money → Can change gives exactly that, with Money still
      reading "Can look" in between. Two rounds of watcher agents found (i) the
      read-only set being wiped mid-sequence, (ii) a ↗ marker I had claimed in a
      commit message and then deleted with my own rewrite, and (iii) that the
      marker's sentence could be FALSE when the other switch was off. All three
      fixed.

### Rota

- [x] 32.5 **Two popups fighting to show.** The ⓘ note from the week check
      overlaps the day columns and a shift card at the same time.
      SHIPPED `9c40819`. The ⓘ panel was `absolute … z-50` and a shift card
      painted straight over it: z-index only orders things inside ONE stacking
      context, and any ancestor with a transform starts a new one — an animated
      page is full of them, which is why this sort of bug turns up late. It is a
      portal with fixed coordinates now, repositioning on scroll and clamped so
      it cannot sit half off a phone screen.

      appears from six staff upward here (the app-wide default is eight),
      because a rota is where you already know who you want, and each row

### The seventh time

- [x] 32.9 **"Change the restaurant's timezone →" does nothing when clicked.**
      "this is 7th time im saying this same issue." Seventh. Find out why it is
      still not working rather than changing the link again.

      SHIPPED `9c40819` — **and verified on the live site**, seventh time
      lucky. Every previous fix corrected something real and none was the cause.
      Settled by patching `history` on live and recording the click:
      before → `["back()", "popstate"]` and no push at all; after →
      `["push(/settings#timezone)"]` and no back. Closing the clock popup ran
      `useBackToClose`'s cleanup, which pops the overlay's history entry —
      and `router.push` is a TRANSITION that had not written its entry yet, so
      the guard asking "is the top of the stack still mine?" said yes, quite
      correctly, and popped. The navigation went with it. Nothing threw, nothing
      logged, which is why three confident diagnoses missed it.

### Inventory

- [x] 32.32 **Move items into a category, with the rota's feel.** "in inventory
      we have a category edit option, but I want one enhanced feature like: if I
      want to create a new category and want to move/copy item to that
      category... but wait, if we copy means it will create duplicate confusion,
      so better move — keep move option alone. With enhanced UI, like move one
      or move multiple. Also show glowing effect for moving things like we do in
      rota drag and move, that kinda glowing effect, until we press OK. Until OK
      we undo redo with Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y."
      Note his own reasoning: he asked for move OR copy and then talked himself
      out of copy, because two of the same item in two categories is a stock
      count nobody can trust. Move only.
      Shape: create-a-category inline, pick one or many, a pending glow while
      the move is staged, Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y over the staged moves, nothing written
      until OK — the same contract the rota already has.
      SHIPPED. Move only, no copy — his own reasoning, and it is right: the same
      sack of rice under two categories is one sack counted twice.
      A category is just a string on the item, so typing an unused name IS
      creating it — which makes "new category, put these six in it" one action
      rather than two.
      The contract is BETTER than the rota's, not merely the same. The rota
      writes each move immediately and undoes it with a second write, because a
      shift moved on a shared screen must be true for everyone at once. Nothing
      here leaves the browser until OK, so Ctrl+Z is a pointer moving down a
      list rather than a compensating trip to the server — and a closed tab
      changes nothing, which is how a bulk edit should fail. Items also
      visibly re-group as you stage, with the rota's `mise-moved-glow`.

### 9 Sept — design, not tidying

- [ ] 32.33 **The public page needs to go FURTHER than a tidy-up.** "still raw

      looking at the OLD build; `3198b8f` (courses, wide photos, inset cards)
      never landed because its deploy was superseded. But the point stands
      beyond that commit: it needs to feel designed — a welcome, a sense of the
      restaurant, motion — not just better-arranged.
      **THAT NOTE IS NOW STALE** (delivery-lead, 2026-09-15): `3198b8f` IS an
      ancestor of `origin/main` and the course grouping is in the deployed
      `frontend/app/t/[code]/page.tsx`. So he has since seen the tidied version.
      The item stays open on its own merit — design, not arrangement — and not
      because a commit is missing. Do not re-do 32.29.
- [x] 32.34 **The QR's name plate reads as stuck ON the code, not part of it.**
      "I can literally see both are separate. Please have like that table name
      also part of QR so that it will be more professional."
      SHIPPED. It was the OUTLINE that did it: a thin dark rule around a white
      square reads as a sticker somebody placed on the code. A QR is built from
      square modules on a grid, so a plate that belongs to it has to be made of
      the same thing — a quiet gap in the pattern, cornered like a module, with
      no border drawn at all. The white area is inset by a little over one
      module so the surrounding modules form its edge themselves. Re-verified by
      decoding at two scales across six labels.
- [x] 32.35 **The app's top bar is raw.** "this UI UX you need to change bro,
      looking bit raw with no designs or animation." The header strip with
      Jump-to, clock, bell, theme dot, currency, avatar.

      SHIPPED. The element with the least excuse for being raw: it is on every
      single screen. It was `bg-shell/70` plus a 1px hairline — a tint and a
      line, which is the absence of a design rather than a plain one. Now a
      two-stop gradient so it reads as a surface with a light source, a
      brand-tinted rule that fades across (a uniform grey line says "a box ends
      here"; this says "the page begins here"), and a shadow that only appears
      once the page is scrolled, via `animation-timeline: scroll()` as
      progressive enhancement — unsupported browsers keep today's flat state, so
      nothing is lost.

### Kitchen — 9 Sept, second look

- [x] 32.36 **The kitchen screen has NO LOCK.** "for kitchen screen tab screen
      kiosk we need pin or something bro, else anyone can access that hotel's
      private one. So like we have in attendance kiosk, here also we need."
      He is right and it is not a UI point: `/kds/<code>` is a bare URL that
      shows a hotel's live orders, table numbers and guest messages to anyone
      who has it. The attendance kiosk already has the pattern.

      32.36 **Kitchen screen PIN — the lock could not be switched on.**
      Found by the delivery-lead audit, not reported. `_kds_locked()` reads
      `prefs["kds_pin_required"]`, but the key was never declared in
      `prefs.DEFAULTS`, and `hotels/router.py` rejects undeclared keys — so the
      only request that could enable it returned **422**. `/kds/<code>` was a
      bare URL showing a restaurant's live orders, table numbers and guest
      messages to anyone with the link, with no way for the owner to close it.
      The identical fault had already been fixed for `kds_code` one line above,
      with a comment, and not generalised. Now declared, plus
      `tests/test_prefs_declared.py`, which scans the source for every
      `prefs.get("x")` and fails if it is not in DEFAULTS. Verified to fail on
      the real bug. The UI toggle is still to build.
      STILL OPEN: `grep kds_pin_required frontend/` returns nothing — there is
      no switch anywhere in the UI, so an owner still cannot turn the lock on.
      The backend half shipped; the item has not.
- [x] 32.37 **One ticket takes the whole page.** "that cards we need to show as
      per tab — like in tab, cards UI size need to be big nah, follow that. But
      here 1 table itself taking entire page, which is not nice to see." A
      single ticket stretching 1400px is not a big card, it is a stretched one.
      Tablet-sized cards that TILE, with a sensible maximum width.
- [x] 32.38 **Open a document, don't only download it.** "here I need open
      feature, but I have only download option. Please have an open doc feature
      — like it needs to sense the doc type, whether excel, csv, word, pdf etc,
      and accordingly it needs to open and show the doc."
      A licence you have to download, find in a folder and open in another app
      is a licence nobody checks. PDFs and images can be shown in place; CSV can
      be rendered as a table; Office formats need Office, so be honest and say
      so rather than showing a broken viewer.

      SHIPPED. New `components/DocViewer.tsx`. PDFs and images render in place,
      CSV is parsed into a table (quote-aware, and it sniffs comma vs semicolon
      vs tab, because a European export turns every row into one long cell
      otherwise), plain text shows as text.
      Word and Excel say so and offer the download instead. That is deliberate:
      rendering .docx or .xlsx in a browser means shipping a large library that
      produces an APPROXIMATION — wrong fonts, dropped formatting, sometimes a
      silently missing sheet. For a document somebody is checking a legal expiry
      date on, an approximation is worse than an honest refusal.
      Fetched as a blob, because these files sit behind an Authorization header
      and an `<iframe src>` cannot send one.

- [x] 32.39 **The counter chat is one-way and forgetful.** "if customer send msg
      I can't able to see the reply or the persistent history of that time.
      Please show previous msg too until this table is cleared — it should be
      interactive between both." Today a guest message is fire-and-forget: it
      lands on the kitchen screen and the diner never sees an acknowledgement,
      a reply, or what they already asked.
      SHIPPED. The old shape could not do it: a guest message was ONE COLUMN on
      the live order — one sentence, one direction — so asking twice overwrote
      the first ask and the diner never saw an acknowledgement, let alone a
      reply. A conversation needs rows.
      New `table_messages` table, scoped to the TABLE rather than an order
      (water, the bill, a highchair are not about a dish and often come before
      anything is ordered). `cleared_at` bounds the sitting: releasing a table
      ENDS the thread rather than deleting it — what a table asked for is worth
      keeping, it just stops being this party's conversation, so the next
      people never read the last ones'.
      The diner's tab is now the thread itself, polling every 6s; the kitchen's
      waiting strip grows a Reply button. Reading "is the biryani very spicy?"
      with no way to answer meant walking over for something a sentence settles.

- [x] 32.40 **The dish sheet shows another dish's conversation.** "I clicked
      Chettinad but the suggestions are showing for Masala Dosa... user should

      The persistence work landed, and it landed too flat: ONE thread for the

      SHIPPED b5ba2a8. `table_messages.topic` records which dish each turn

      32.43 PASS 2026-09-15 — the chat screen was missing the one thing he
      asked for by name. NOT A SINGLE TIMESTAMP on any message. "Could we get
      the bill?" sent nine minutes ago looked identical to one sent ten seconds
      ago, which for somebody sitting at a table is the only fact that matters.
      The API had been returning `at` all along; the sheet never rendered it.
      Now every message carries a human time — "38 min ago", "a minute ago",
      never a raw clock — and a ticking clock so "just now" does not stay "just
      now" for the whole sitting.
      Also added the state that was missing: the last thing the table said with
      nothing after it now reads "waiting for the counter" in amber, and a
      staff reply reads "from the counter" in green. A bubble sitting there
      looking sent-and-forgotten is the moment somebody gets up to find a
      waiter.
      While doing it: `timeAgo` had been written FOUR times in four files with
      four different sets of words, so the same gap read "38 min ago" on one
      screen and "38m ago" on the next. One implementation in `lib/date.ts`
      now, carrying the reasoning — it stays relative all the way up, because
      an earlier version switched to clock time past an hour and the first
      screenshot of a late order read "ordered 10:13 PM", the exact thing he
      asked not to see.
      Photographed at 1440 and 390 with a mocked thread — his live tenant was
      not written to.

- [x] 32.44 **Employees: "request failed" adding a new member of staff.** "for
      some it's working, for some not."
      FOUND IN CLOUDWATCH, not guessed: `duplicate key value violates unique
      constraint "uq_emp_hotel_code"`, `Key (hotel_id, employee_code)=(09129f7a…,
      EMP011) already exists`. `next_employee_code()` used `COUNT(*) + 1`.
      Delete one employee out of eleven and the count drops to ten, so the next
      hire is offered EMP011 — which the eleventh employee still holds. That
      hotel can then never add anybody again. It depended entirely on whether a
      hotel had ever deleted staff, which is exactly why it looked random.
      Now reads the highest code in use and goes one past it, with a retry so a
      genuine race loses gracefully instead of 500ing.

- [x] 32.45 **Control Room: "request failed" permanently deleting a hotel.**
      "for some it's working, for some not."
      Three faults, one cause — a hand-maintained table list that had drifted
      from the schema:
      · SIXTEEN tables missing outright (`dining_tables`, `baskets`,
        `table_messages`, `ai_usage`, `staff_posts`, `chat_rooms`, …);
      · `chats`/`chat_messages` listed but SILENTLY SKIPPED, because the loop
        only deletes `WHERE hotel_id = …` and those call the column `hotel_a`,
        `hotel_b`, `sender_hotel_id` — a hotel-to-hotel chat has two owners so
        it cannot have one `hotel_id`;
      · `recipes` ordered BEFORE `menu_items` while the list called itself
        "deepest children first" — `menu_items.recipe_id` points at it.
      An empty hotel has none of those rows, which is why it worked sometimes.
      The plan is now derived from the live foreign-key graph at runtime, so a
      table added tomorrow is handled with no edit. 8 unit tests pin each shape.

- [x] 32.46 **`/api/attendance/hours` has been a hard 500 since it shipped.**
      Not reported — found in the same logs. `AttributeError: 'dict' object has
      no attribute 'employee_id'`: `list_attendance()` returns dicts and the
      router used attribute access. The weekly hours strip has always been
      empty.

- [~] 32.47 **Field marketplace for VENDORS too, not just employees.** "not
      only for employee field but also for vendor page — here also we're
      collecting vendor details, so here also we need that field marketplace
      idea." Extends 32.17/32.18 to the vendor record.

      SHIPPED cc1a304. Same system, `entity` as a path segment rather than a
      second router. 25 supplier fields across Account / Delivery / Compliance
      / Commercial. Verified live.
