## 48.4 — DELETE EVERYTHING, with a way back

    "i need delete all datas feature...like it wont delete the owner login
     alone...other thatn this..it will delete litrelly all the datas...makr it
     like dangerous"

> ⚠️ **PREREQUISITE FOUND AND FIXED FIRST — `247a74c` (queued).** The wipe
> could not ship as it stood: `archive()` walked a hand-typed table list while
> `purge()` derives its tables from the live foreign-key graph, so the backup
> was a SUBSET of the delete. `audit_events`, `chats`, `dining_tables`,
> `baskets` and `ai_usage` were being destroyed and never saved. That is the
> worst possible shape for this feature — it looks like it worked and only
> fails when somebody needs it back.

- [x] 48.4a wipe every record for a hotel, keeping ONLY the owner login
      `POST /hotels/wipe` — derived from the same delete plan as the purge, so
      it cannot drift from it. Logins and the hotel row stay, so he is still
      signed in afterwards looking at an empty product
- [x] 48.4b presented as dangerous — this is the most destructive control we own
      `DangerZone.tsx`, last on the Settings page. Three deliberate acts: open
      it, type the restaurant's own NAME (read off the screen, so it cannot be
      muscle memory), type your password
- [x] 48.4c ⭐ **A SNAPSHOT IS TAKEN BEFORE THE WIPE, AND THE CONTROL ROOM CAN
      PUT IT BACK.** `RestoreHotel.tsx` on the Control Room hotel page, above
      the delete control — recovery sits above destruction
- [x] 48.4d **no snapshot, no delete.** If the archive fails the endpoint
      answers 503 and nothing is removed. Tested
- [x] 48.4e three refusals on the restore: a snapshot from a different
      restaurant, an unreadable format, and a restaurant that still has data in
      it — restoring on top would give two of everything
- [x] 48.4f ⭐ **the restore can actually insert what the archive saved.** The
      snapshot is JSON, so `default=str` turns every timestamp, date, Decimal
      and UUID into a string, and asyncpg refuses a `str` where the statement
      says `timestamptz` — the restore would have died on the first table with
      a `created_at`, which is nearly all of them. Values now go in as text with
      an explicit SQL `CAST` to each column's real type, read from `pg_attribute`
- [x] 48.4g the S3 read/write sit behind two seams so the wipe→restore ROUND
      TRIP is tested against a real database. Inline boto3 meant the most
      important path in the product could only ever have been exercised for the
      first time by an owner who needed it
- [ ] 48.4h photographed working on live


## 51 — the wall tablet, live (2026-09-23)

Two faults photographed on `nirai1.dineai.cloud/kiosk` and the attendance
settings, plus one new feature request.

### 51a — "why i cant see rota...off... whats happening??"

Both wall panels opened to **"Could not reach DineAI."**

Not an outage. CloudWatch, `/dineai/app`, every 60 seconds:

    GET /api/rota/shifts -> 403
    user=kiosk+d0000000-...@kiosk.dineai.cloud

`KioskPanel` called `/rota/shifts` and `/employees/leave/list`, both gated on
`employees:read` — the permission the kiosk deliberately does **not** hold,
because it returns `monthly_salary`, `ni_number`, `bank_sort_code` and
`bank_account_no` to a tablet unlocked by a door PIN. The panels were built
against endpoints their own credential is forbidden to call.

> **"Built but unreachable", instance 8.** Every previous one was a button
> whose listener was never mounted. This is the same shape one layer down: a
> CALLER wired to a callee it cannot reach. The events gate I added catches the
> DOM version of this and would never have caught this one.

- [x] 51a.1 `GET /attendance/kiosk-board` — one call, gated on
      `employees:roster` (names only), which the kiosk holds and every manager
      satisfies anyway
- [x] 51a.2 returns **only** name + one detail line. No salary, no NI, no bank
- [x] 51a.3 the owner's show/hide is enforced **server-side**. It previously
      decided only whether a BUTTON was drawn — the data was never protected by
      it, so a stale browser could have displayed leave the owner switched off
- [x] 51a.4 the panel reports what the server said. "Could not reach DineAI"
      for a 403 is a network story told about a refusal, and it sent me looking
      at the wrong thing
- [x] 51a.5 regression tests: the board is readable *with a real kiosk token*,
      and a switched-off panel serves `null`

### 51b — "why everytime it asking to create pin"

    "let use previous pin...if needed means we can create new pin..
     pelaes chnage this UI alos"

Two causes, and the first one is why the UI behaved that way:

1. **The PIN was hashed and nothing else**, so the panel was physically unable
   to answer "what is my PIN?". Offering a new one was the only move it had.
2. **One form, two jobs.** `pin` was REQUIRED on `/lock/pin`, and the display
   toggles lived in the same form — so opening it to tick "show the rota"
   pushed a brand new PIN through and invalidated the code taped to the tablet.

- [x] 51b.1 a second, **encrypted** copy of the PIN (Fernet, key derived from
      the app secret). The hash stays and is still the only thing `verify()`
      reads — authentication is unchanged
- [x] 51b.2 `POST /attendance/lock/reveal` — owner only, password re-checked.
      Being shown a door code and being able to change one are the same
      capability from the point of view of somebody at an unattended screen
- [x] 51b.3 honest about the one case it cannot answer: a PIN set before this
      existed was only ever hashed, so it says so instead of showing a blank
- [x] 51b.4 display settings save **on the spot**, no PIN and no password
- [x] 51b.5 absent `pin` = settings only; **empty** `pin` is still a 400.
      Collapsing those two would turn a rejected PIN into a silent success
- [x] 51b.6 UI: step 1 reads "Your PIN is already set — keep using it" once one
      exists. "Show the PIN" is a normal button; "Replace it with a new PIN" is
      a quiet underline, with a warning that the tablet will be locked out
- [x] 51b.7 photographed working on live — `v0923-pin-panel.png`: "Your PIN is already set", reveal works

### 47.3 / 47.4 / 50f.6 — how the drill-down actually works (2026-09-23)

Three levels, one scene, one camera:

1. the platform — every restaurant as a bubble, area = share of traffic
2. **inside a restaurant** — the areas it actually opened, which SUM to it.
   That summation is the whole justification for drawing them nested
3. **inside an area** — a popup with the two doors, because they answer
   different questions:
   - *Open <them> in the Control Room* — the operator's view OF them
   - *View as them → /inventory* — their actual screen, on a 15-minute
     read-only support token

- [x] an area with no screen behind it gets **no door**, not a door onto a 404.
      A link that lands nowhere is worse than no link, because it looks like
      the feature works
- [x] the support view can now land on a named page rather than the dashboard
- [x] ⚠️ **that destination is validated on arrival, not trusted.** It travels
      in a URL hash, and `location.replace` on an unchecked value is an open
      redirect — `//evil.example` is protocol-relative and leaves the site.
      One leading slash, letters and dashes, nothing else
- [x] Escape undoes ONE thing: the popup first, then the level
- [x] photographed working on live (duplicate: the live proof is tracked as 56.4)

## 54 — the AWS budgets, actually fixed (2026-09-23)

All three faults were real and all three are corrected on the live account.
The budgets are **not** Terraform-managed (`infra/iam.tf` only grants read),
so the API change is durable and will not be reverted by an apply.

**43.7 — `IncludeCredit: true` on both.** Credits were counted as spend
cancelling spend, so `ActualSpend` read $0.00 and would have kept reading
$0.00 until the credits died — the one day a runway warning is worthless.
Now `false` on both, and the numbers appeared immediately:

| budget | before | after | limit |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mise-bedrock-ai` | $0.00 | **$1.64** | $10 |
| `mise-monthly-all` | $0.00 | **$25.81** | $40 (65%) |

That $25.81 is gross usage covered by credits — out-of-pocket is still £0.
It is the runway figure, which is what was asked for.

**43.6 — the Bedrock filter matched nothing.** It named `Claude 3.7 Sonnet
(Amazon Bedrock Edition)` and `Amazon Bedrock`. Cost Explorer, checked today
rather than copied from this file, bills under **`Claude Sonnet 4.6 (Amazon
Bedrock Edition)`**. Filter now lists Sonnet 4.6, Haiku 4.5, Opus 4.6 and
`Amazon Bedrock`.

> ⚠️ **THIS STILL ROTS** and listing three models only slows it down. A budget
> filter cannot match a substring, so the day we move model it empties again —
> and putting the entry tier on Haiku is a deliberate plan. The budget is the
> BACKSTOP. The durable guard is the dashboard, which matches
> `"bedrock" in service.lower()`.

**43.10 — the alerting was backwards.** Three ACTUAL alerts could never fire
while ActualSpend was pinned at $0.00; meanwhile `FORECASTED > 25%` sat in
ALARM permanently, because a forecast always exceeds a quarter of its budget.
Four dead, one crying wolf — which trains you to ignore all five.

- [x] 54.1 the three ACTUAL alerts now work. 25% and 50% are correctly in
      ALARM at 65%; 80% reads OK
- [x] 54.2 `FORECASTED > 25%` raised to **90%** rather than deleted, so the
      subscriber survives and the signal means something. It reads OK now
- [x] 54.3 (expired heads-up: the date passed; told him as an FYI on 21 Nov) ⚠️ **he will start getting budget emails.** That is the point — the
      alerts were silent for months — but it is a change he should not have to
      work out from an inbox

Audited rather than assumed: **31 raw `<select>` elements across 18 files**,
next to a shared `<Select>` that already has the popover, the ✓, the search
box above eight options and the flip-up when there is no room below. So the
complaint was exact.

Two props were missing from the shared one, and that is *why* several places
were still native: `disabled` and an accessible label. Both added — a picker
with no visible label was announcing itself as an unlabelled button.

## 58 — THE BUSINESS REPORT, built from his sample (2026-09-23)

    "i need one consolidated super spceial export feature in pnl area that
     includes litrelly al thre details like expense , sales, money ectetc (for
     particluar days or whatever we choose)"
    "have in excel, csv, pdf, words in all fomr we need to allwo user to
     dnwload... please use cool colors and decorotaew with stuff"
    "also have a cusotmised feature like ask user whther to iunclude tis
     calcuateion or not or leave empty to keep default"

His sample — `docs/Nirai_August_2026_Updated_MBR_v2.pdf` — is the spec, and
its shape is the point: **not one P&L table, but a stack of titled sections,
each with its own total, and a Notes column allowed to say "Not supplied"
rather than print a confident zero.**

- [x] 58.1 **seven sections**, the ones his report has that our data can
      answer: the headline numbers, delivery platforms, day by day, categories,
      suppliers, staff cost, services & utilities
- [x] 58.2 ⭐ **per-platform, which our P&L never showed.** It flattened every
      channel into one "commission" line — and a restaurant does not negotiate
      with "delivery commission", it negotiates with Deliveroo. Gross, received,
      their cut and the effective %, per channel, exactly like his page 1
- [x] 58.3 **all four formats** — PDF, Excel, Word, CSV. Word because it is the
      one somebody EDITS before sending on; a PDF is final and a spreadsheet is
      the wrong shape for a paragraph
- [x] 58.4 one structure, four renderers, so a new section appears in all four
      at once instead of three of them drifting a month behind
- [x] 58.5 Excel gets **a sheet per section** with frozen headers — seven
      stacked tables on one sheet cannot be sorted without wrecking each other
- [x] 58.6 **numbers go into Excel as numbers.** "£1,234.00" as text is a
      spreadsheet you cannot sum, which is most of why anyone wanted Excel
- [x] 58.7 the picker: **everything on by default, untick to remove.** Empty is
      how somebody says "the usual", not how they ask for a blank document —
      and the server agrees, so both halves mean the same thing
- [x] 58.8 **it previews before it downloads.** A report is the easiest thing
      in the world to download, open, and find is the wrong three weeks
- [x] 58.9 ⭐ **a figure we cannot work out prints as an em dash, never 0.00.**
      The honesty in his own report is the most valuable thing in it
- [x] 58.10 ⚠️ **one place we CANNOT match him, and the report says so.**
      `commission_pct` is NOT NULL defaulting to 0, so a platform nobody has
      entered a rate for is indistinguishable from cash, which genuinely costs
      nothing. His report writes "Not supplied"; we cannot, so the section
      carries the caveat in print rather than inventing the number
- [x] 58.11 **proved on live by authenticated download**, not by a screenshot —
      for a file export that is the stronger evidence. All four came back with
      the right magic bytes: PDF 4.8 KB `%PDF`, Excel 11.4 KB `PK`, Word 38.4 KB
      `PK`, CSV 1.8 KB with a UTF-8 BOM. The Word one mattered most: `python-docx`
      is a new dependency imported lazily, so a 200 on the route would not have
      proved the image actually has it
- [x] 58.13 the content is his shape — headline numbers with Notes, the
      per-platform table (FoodHub 5%, Cash 0%, Online Orders 0%) with the 0%
      caveat printed, day by day, categories, supplier payments
- [x] 58.14 ⭐ **reading it found a bug the tests did not.** August is a loss and
      it printed `£-1,465.01` — symbol before the minus, which reads as a typo.
      Every statement a restaurant sees writes `-£1,465.01`. Fixed, with a test
- [x] 58.12 the **reserves / not-P&L** section from his page 3 (rent reserve,
      chef salary reserve). Needs a real "this is a transfer, not an expense"
      flag on expense categories — deliberately NOT faked by matching category
      names, which is the silently-wrong pattern this product cannot afford

    "in vendor page for each vednor i need confolsitead report of what we

     last month last 2month tecet...i need a export featrue we can eport as

Open a supplier → **📊 What we buy from them**.

- [x] 59.1 five sections: the headline numbers, what we buy from them, every

- [x] 59.2 **periods as presets** — this month, last month, last 2 / 3 / 6
      months, this year, everything, plus a custom range. "last month last
      2month tecet" is how the question is actually asked; making somebody work
      out that last month started on the 1st is how a feature goes unused
- [x] 59.3 **seen first, downloaded second**, which is the order he asked for
- [x] 59.4 all four formats, from the **same renderers as the business
      report** — so the two documents look like they came from one company,
      because they did. He asked for three; Word arrives free
- [x] 59.5 ⭐ **a DRAFT order is not a purchase.** It is a shopping list nobody
      has sent, and counting it would tell a restaurant it owed money it has
      not committed. Only SENT and RECEIVED count, and the report says so
- [x] 59.6 ⭐ **ordered and paid are separate answers**, shown side by side with
      the difference. One "total" that quietly means one of them is how a
      supplier page starts misleading people

      line is the thing actually worth asking a supplier about
- [x] 59.8 a supplier that is not yours answers **404, the same as one that

      restaurant's suppliers
- [x] 59.9 the literal `/vendors/report/sections` is declared **before**
      `/vendors/{vendor_id}` and a test holds it there. Starlette matches in
      declaration order and this project has been bitten by that already

      returned with correct magic bytes for a real supplier, and the content is
      right: Ordered £951.21 across 13 orders, Paid £464.18, **Difference
      £487.03** — the gap a single "total" would have hidden — plus per-item
      quantities with cheapest and dearest unit prices

## 61 — SALES: the past was invisible, and the float stopped carrying (2026-09-29)

    "if i enter today's sales and money box....yesterday sales related history
     i cant able to see even if i go back using time...... please be persistant
     also i can also edit that sales information if needed which will also sync
     whereever needed"
    "once time is 00:00 i mean 12am then the yesterday's sales closing amount
     (i mean the money box final amt) need to be showing as today's starting
     amount"
    "that sales entering area is tooo strechted which is bit old styel...pelase
     think deeply"

**Evidence first.** CloudWatch showed my usual checks were looking at the wrong
restaurant: his real one is **NIRAI.Reading** (`niraireading@gmail.com`), not
the test tenant. On 26 Sep he saved takings for the 25th and 26th — every write
201/200. So nothing was lost. The database said what WAS wrong:

| day | opening | counted | closed |
|---|---|---|---|
| 13 Aug | 196.90 | 313.00 | ✓ |
| 14 Aug | **313.00** | 339.00 | ✓ |
| 15 Aug | **339.00** | 237.85 | ✓ |
| 16 Aug | **237.85** | 282.70 | ✓ |
| 25 Sep | 123.00 | — | never |
| 26 Sep | 123.00 | — | never |

**The carry was correct and refused to work.** August carries to the penny
because each day was counted. `carried_opening` returned None for any
uncounted day — and no day since 8 Sep was counted.

**The nightly auto-close that should have counted them crashed every night it
had work**: `NoReferencedTableError: cash_events.changed_by could not find
table 'users'`. Run standalone by systemd it never imported the User model, so
the first cash-event write failed. On empty nights it wrote nothing, so systemd
logged "Finished" and nobody saw it. **It also only looked at "yesterday"**, so
one missed night stranded that day permanently.

**A past day looked empty.** The takings area was an ADD form — blank 0.00
boxes — and the saved figures sat in a list below titled "Today's lines" on
every day. Reproduced on 8 Sep: tiles said £32.85, all 8 boxes were empty.

### fixed

- [x] 61.1 ⭐ **the float carries at 00:00, live.** An uncounted day carries its
      EXPECTED close, labelled "estimated" in amber — the same number the 1am
      job would write, true at midnight instead of whenever a timer runs
- [x] 61.2 it carries from the last day that **traded**, so a Monday closure no
      longer resets Tuesday to zero; an auto count is carried as an estimate too
- [x] 61.3 bounded at 7 uncounted days — past that it is a guess about a guess,
      and an unbounded walk back is recursion with no floor
- [x] 61.4 the money-box label says **which day** it carried from and whether
      it was counted or estimated, instead of "yesterday's closing count" always
- [x] 61.5 ⭐ **the nightly job loads every model** (discovered, not listed —
      a hand list is what drifts) and **recovers every missed night in the last
      7 days, oldest first**, so each close feeds the next day's opening
- [x] 61.6 ⭐ **a past day shows its figures**, in each channel's card, with the
      day named in words — "Takings for Thursday 25 September"
- [x] 61.7 ⭐ **any saved figure can be corrected in place** — tap the amount,
      tap how it was paid. `PATCH /sales/days/{day}/lines/{id}`, same id, audited
- [x] 61.8 **edits sync.** Reports read the same rows, so the edit is the sync —
      except an AUTO-closed day, whose guessed count now follows its takings. A
      HUMAN count never does: changing a sale does not change what was counted
- [x] 61.9 removing a sale is now audited. Adding was; removing was not, so
      money could leave the books with no record of who took it out
- [x] 61.10 **"Paid by" is per channel.** It was one control for every box, so
      Deliveroo typed while CASH was selected went in as cash — and inflated
      the expected drawer. Delivery platforms now default to ONLINE
- [x] 61.11 drafts reset on a day change — typed figures used to save onto
      whichever day you had stepped to
- [x] 61.12 a retired channel's PAST sales still show on the days it has them
- [x] 61.13 the day stepper is no longer hidden from read-only users — the
      whole card was gated on write access, so an accountant could not move a day
- [x] 61.14 **the layout**: compact channel cards, 3 across on desktop, with the
      box INSIDE the card next to the name. ⚠️ Not popups — he rejected tiles
      once because each opened a popup; the rule from that stands: every box
      visible, typed directly, saved once

### the test lesson, again

- [x] 61.15 the existing autoclose tests were **green the whole time production
      crashed**, because the test process imports the whole app. The new test
      runs the job in a FRESH process the way systemd does
- [x] 61.16 ⚠️ **and my first version of that test also passed without the
      fix.** It called `configure_mappers()`; the crash is in the flush, which
      resolves every foreign key. Measured: without the fix, `users` is not
      registered and **7 foreign keys cannot resolve**. The test now resolves
      them directly — passes with the fix, **fails without it**, both checked

### still to do

- [x] 61.17 verified on live by a QA agent with Playwright, read-only, on the
      test tenant. **A** past day (8 Sept) reads "Takings for Tuesday 8
      September", 3 saved figures in the cards, no "Today's lines" anywhere.
      **C** 3 cards across at 1280px, 1 at 390px, zero sideways scroll inside
      the card. **D** today reads "Today's takings". No console errors
- [x] 61.19 ⚠️ **B FAILED on live, and it was mine.** The opening showed
      £2,200 (carried from 5 Sept, never counted) with NO "estimated" label —
      a guess presented as a count, the exact thing this section was built to
      stop. The page decided "was it carried?" by asking whether `opening_cash`
      was non-zero; the server has been writing the carried float straight
      into `opening_cash`, so that branch always won and the label never
      rendered. Very likely the OLD "carried from yesterday" line was dead the
      same way, before today. Now keyed off the server's own flag,
      `suggested_opening`, which is non-null exactly when the opening is a carry
- [x] 61.20 **the label, re-verified on live after `88a4647` — PASS**, and the
      screenshot looked at, not just the assertion: "↩ carried from Sat 5 Sept
      — estimated, that day's cash was never counted. Count the drawer to be
      sure." in amber (`rgb(180,83,9)` — the light theme's remap of amber-300,
      which is near-invisible on white). Opening 2200.00; expected in drawer
      £2,232.85 = 2,200 + 32.85 cash sales. Control day 2 Aug has a TYPED
      opening, so correctly shows no carry line
- [ ] 61.21 the agent's mobile full-page screenshot shows the fixed bottom nav
      over the Deliveroo card — probably a capture artifact of a fixed element,
      flagged rather than dismissed until a real phone scroll confirms it
- [x] 61.18 (expired heads-up: the date passed; told him as an FYI on 21 Nov) ⚠️ **tonight's run will auto-close his 25th and 26th** at their
      expected cash, labelled as estimates. That is what the job was always meant
      to do and could not; he should know before he sees it

⚠️ **Rejected in §46, §47, §50f and now here.** Every previous attempt was
built from a guess about what he meant. This time a product designer reviews
the LIVE screen first — screenshots at 1440/1280/390, all three drill levels,
the history of every rejection — and the build follows the diagnosis, not the
other way round.

Visible in the code before the review, and very likely part of "tight":
- the page claims "every pixel of the window is map", but the map sits in a
  **fixed 68vh card**, below a view switch and above a legend strip
- **two views behind a switch**, left there "while he decides" — for weeks
- the legend explains **hexagons and diamonds that only exist in the other view**
- the stats bar is **one run-on sentence** of five figures

- [x] 62.1 designer's diagnosis, from 41 live screenshots at 1440/1280/390:
      **structural, not cosmetic.** Tight in the middle and empty everywhere
      else (a 550px circle in a 1388px canvas, a 180px dead band below); 8 of
      17 bubbles unnamed at 1440, 14 of 17 at 390; no order, and positions that
      moved between loads; a thick black focus SQUARE round a round bubble; a
      bill ring nobody could read; and the rejected Columns view telling him
      MORE than the pretty one
- [x] 62.2 direction: **ordered orbits on a dark stage.** Ring = state (busy ·
      quiet · not started), clockwise from 12 = rank. The same pattern at every
      level. Chosen over treemap (rectangles — he asked for bubbles), sunburst
      (a circle again, unreadable past 12), force/constellation (moves on every
      load) and the circle-pack it replaces (no order, no room for names)
- [ ] 62.3 built, then verified on live at desktop and phone width

- [x] 62.8 pulses along the spokes, faster for busier restaurants — the one kind

### 62 — proving it before it shipped

It was rendered LOCALLY against the real live data (Playwright playing the
API, since production's CORS refuses localhost) and looked at, at 1440 and
390, at all three levels. That caught eight problems before he could:

- [x] 62.14 the busiest name clipped ("NIRAI · n…"); a two-line name plate
      running under the biggest sphere; corner captions cut off at both screen
      edges; a dim "credits covered" chip
- [x] 62.15 ⚠️ **my first overlap fix made it worse**: modelling a name plate as a
      fat circle shrank the busiest sphere from 53px to 28px. A plate is now a
      RECTANGLE under the sphere, and a collision is cured by **turning the
      ring**, not by shrinking the map
- [x] 62.16 ring labels hidden under a sphere ("QUIET · 6" behind pppk) — the
      label is now an obstacle the ring turns around
- [x] 62.17 plates running off the stage edge on a phone — the edge is now an
      obstacle too
- [x] 62.18 ⚠️ **a feedback loop on phones**: the drawing sat in the flow, so the
      stage measured itself, sized the drawing, and the drawing made the stage
      taller — 1,273px on an 844px phone, with bodies moving under the finger.
      The drawing is now positioned out of flow and the top bar is MEASURED
- [x] 62.19 on a phone the ring names go in a legend row, and the top band is
      reserved so nothing is placed under the legend or the door buttons
- [x] 62.20 when a stage is genuinely too tight for two-line plates, small
      bodies keep their NAME and drop the number to the hover card. The name is
      never dropped
- [x] 62.21 **0 overlaps** on the live data at 1440×688, 1280×590, 1024×560,
      768×700, 390×550 and 390×640, platform and inside a restaurant
- [ ] 62.22 a small 360px phone on a very short stage still has 3 — recorded,
      not hidden. **Then measured properly**: a sweep of 5 phone widths × 18
      stage heights × 4 fleet sizes found the live map collided at **34 of 90
      phone sizes**, not one — spot checks had hidden it. Three changes, each
      proven by the sweep: (a) layouts are ranked by sphere size too, so a
      "zero overlaps" won by shrinking every sphere to half no longer wins;
      (b) a corner body moves to the bottom when the outer ring's crown is
      under it; (c) a name near the edge SLIDES IN like a tooltip; plus the
      phone ring spacing re-chosen by the sweep. Result: 0 overlaps at every
      size on live data (278 → 12 across all four fleets, the rest only on a
      420px-tall stage). Rendered and looked at 1440/1024/390/375/360. Ticked
      when seen on live
- [ ] 62.23 **retire the old pieces once he approves**: BubbleMap.tsx,
      NeuralMap.tsx and the tall sheet are no longer used by this route
- [x] 62.24 READ/WRITE split per area (needs a backend `group_by(method)`,
      declared on the schema), and `last_seen` per restaurant so the rings can
      mean "active this week" rather than a share of the period
- [x] 62.25 ⚠️ **a TAP left a restaurant stuck "hovered"** — found in the live
      390px screenshots of 19f1d8f, not by review. A finger fires
      `pointerenter` and never `pointerleave`, so after tapping in and back out
      every other body stayed dimmed and a hover card hung off the right edge.
      Hover now follows a MOUSE only (`pointerType === "mouse"`), and the card
      flips to the left of the body when it would cross the stage edge.
      Proven locally with a real touch device (tap → Escape → 0 dimmed, no
      card). **Live, d821ca8**: a real touch at 390×844 — tap nirai1, Escape
      → 0 dimmed, 0 cards; desktop hover card's right edge 1184 of 1440, gone
      when the mouse leaves. Screenshots looked at

## 64 — two money-accuracy fixes from the pending list (2026-09-29)

- [x] 64.1 **every closed month on the money page said "1st → 1st".**
      Compaction (§45.8) rolls a finished month into one row dated the 1st,
      and the period was read back as min(day)..max(day) — the same date. A
      closed month now spans its whole month; the open month keeps its real,
      partial span. Found 21 Sep, never entered until the 29 Sep review
- [x] 64.2 ⭐ **expenses created by a purchase order were editable and
      unlabelled.** Two faults, one behind the other:
      · `purchase_order_id` was declared on ExpenseOut and **never put in the
        row**, so it reached the page as null for EVERY expense — the page
        could not label what it was never told
      · the "edit it there" rule for payroll existed **only on the screen**.

### 73 — progress, 2 Nov

The usage limit cut every builder off mid-flight (twice). Nothing was lost:
each worktree's partial work was carried over as a PATCH (never a merge — the
worktrees sit on the pre-purge history, and a merge would bring the photos back)
and finished by the main session.

- 71 party price — reviewed; the one real finding FIXED: per-plate cost was
  rounded to 2dp before × plates (0.125 × 500 = 65.00 instead of 62.50); now
  4dp per plate, money totals 2dp. Infinity refused on all three numbers. The
  page rounds each plate once, half-up like the server, so screen, PDF and
  saved quote agree. Tests added for both. (Shared currency symbols — Rs, $ —
  are an older design gap, logged, not widened here.)
- 70 pay later — the second builder had FINISHED: settle() for every path,
  mark-paid, lump payments oldest-first, cash rules, 28 tests, both receive
  buttons, scan-bill choice, PO badge/filter, vendor page. Under adversarial
  review before it ships
- 32.10–32.13 bell — backend pipeline + state table + 13 pipeline tests were
  done; the PANEL built here (boxes per area, pinned danger rows that cannot
  be dismissed or muted, +N more, mute/unmute, put away + undo, urgent-only,
  server-side read state with a one-time hand-over of the old local "seen"),
  plus 4 tests that read/put-away is per person and per restaurant
- 58.12 reserves — backend was complete (16 tests); the Expenses page now
  offers "This money is: spent / set aside" and a per-category switch that
  says out loud it moves past months, with the server's refusal shown inside
  the popup
- 43.8 AI cost — PARTIAL (cache tokens captured, unlogged AI paths found and
  logged); allocation + tests + screen still to do → next deploy
- Migrations chained to ONE head: …4b877dcec454 → ad09329a5455 (party) →
  4f17f04452ef (pay later) → 8c43d7871e59 (bell) → d72b0b20f521 (reserves).
  Whole app imports; mappers configure; every FK resolves; OpenAPI builds
  (404 paths) with every new route present
- 70 pay later — the adversarial review said "do not ship", and was right.
  All six findings FIXED, each with a test that fails if it comes back:
  · H1 a stock movement COMMITTED mid-receive, releasing the order's row
    lock after line 1 — a double tap on "Received & paid" could add the stock
    twice and pay twice. record_movement(commit=False); one commit at the end
  · H2 with "post purchases to expenses" OFF, cash reached the till ZERO times.
    The supplier page books the whole cash payment to the chosen category again
    (and keeps the picker visible); cash through an order is refused, plainly
  · M1 "Received & paid" needed only receive rights — now pay rights too
    (server + button)
  · M2 scan-bill "Received & paid" paid the ORDER price, not the bill's: the
    bill's prices are written onto the order line before it is valued
  · M3 a payment could predate the delivery and pull food into an earlier
    month: refused, and a lump payment settles only orders already arrived
  · L1 "to pay" rounds per line like the order total; L2 an empty "Received &
    paid" is refused instead of locking the order forever

## 74 — one employee's attendance Excel never downloaded (4 Nov)

    "in attendance section -> when i try to download excel of particular
     employee im clicking multiple times but still it not getting downloaded"

- [x] 74.1 CAUSE, from CloudWatch: 25 clicks in a minute on NIRAI.Reading, every
      one a 422 in ~10ms. The button called /attendance/history/<id>.xlsx — a
      route that never existed — so "<id>.xlsx" hit the /{employee_id} route and
      failed to parse as a UUID; a `void` on the promise hid the failure. It now
      calls the existing /attendance/range.xlsx?employee_id=, says "Downloading…",
      and shows an error if one happens. All 47 download URLs in the app were
      checked against the real routes: this was the only dead one

      "<name>-attendance.xlsx", a real workbook. "This year": Balaji 7 rows,
      Mohamed 5, pandian 4, Praveen 3, Rajkumar 2, Sundar 1, each file holding
      ONLY that person; the 3 with no attendance get a valid empty file

## 80 — What the live payroll check found (4 Nov)

Live 3664b45, nirai1, 1440 and 390 wide, nothing written: order, totals (computed
from the rows, both months add up), four popups, sidebar links, preview, refusal
shown inside the sheet, "Email payslips" cancelled with no request sent. Those
pass, so 76.1–76.5 are ticked below only once this round is on live too.

- [x] 80.1 ⚠️ MONEY: running a period again overwrote settled payslips. PAID went
      back to DRAFT with a new amount, while the expense booked at approval
      stayed at the old one, and the preview offered "pay £200" to someone
      already paid. Approved or paid payslips are now left alone: by name it is
      refused in words, a whole-month run carries on and recalculates only the
      drafts and says how many it left, the preview warns. To redo one, remove
      it (which reverses the expense). Found by reading the code, never run on
      live. Whether this already happened to real payslips is UNKNOWN:
      CloudWatch (from 29 Jul) holds only 11 payroll requests and no successful
      approve or pay at all, though PAID rows exist, so it is not a full record.
      `audit_events` (payroll.run after payroll.approve) would answer it: a
      read-only production query, his call, not yet run
- [x] 80.2 spaces lost across the app, 30 sentences on 10 screens. A compiler bug
      in Next 16.2.7: JSX text after an element loses its leading space when it
      contains an entity and a line break. The source reads correctly, so review
      cannot see it, and earlier reports of this ("paid below", "×4.12apart")
      were wrongly dismissed as misreads. `scripts/check-jsx-spaces.mjs` finds
      and fixes it
- [x] 80.3 the gates claimed to run on every deploy never did: check-sections and
      check-events were in no workflow. They now run inside `npm run lint`, with
      the new check, which CI and the deploy both run
- [x] 80.4 the page moved behind every popup on a phone: `overflow-x: clip` on
      html stopped the lock reaching the viewport. Shared, so this fixes every
      popup in the app. Proven by mouse wheel; a finger drag is not yet proven
- [x] 80.5 the mic sat on the Net pay figure on a phone. It stands down while
      anything covers the page, on phones only, so desktop keeps voice in forms
- [x] 80.6 a refused form said "Request failed". It now gives the server's reason,
      across the app
- [x] 80.7 negative Net to pay no longer shows green · "−£0.00" gone · short
      labels so all four jobs fit a phone
- Not changed: sidebar sub-items are collapsed until their section is opened or

## 88 — ⚠️ I broke the dish photos, fixed the same day (5 Nov)

      name, assuming ".jpg", which made `chicken-curry..webp`. tsc, lint and CI all

His words, gathered from four messages so nothing is dropped: "mobile: in top some buttons

giving a movement like zoom in… this i want in our default UI too… just zooming in, no need
zoom out… note it down in checklist… don't miss anything"

Measured before any change (22 Nov, GPU headless, scripted scroll): 0.3 frames a second on
the Chain design; 13.5 with the card chrome hidden; 21.5 with every animation off. The
document was ~2,000 px taller than the site (the spine pulses' transform).

Smoothness and bugs (first)

      conic border), no permanent animations on every card, no backdrop blur on turning
      cards, far fewer GPU layers — measured before and after
- [x] 94.2 "empty cards" after a reload: what is inside a card follows the CARD's scroll
      position (a card that clips its edges is its own scroll box, so its children's own
      view timelines could stick at invisible)
- [x] 94.3 the page scrolls past its end into an empty area: nothing may stretch the
      document (the divider's travelling pulses moved a full-height box)
- [x] 94.4 phone: top bar icons missing (mail, Instagram) — find out why, fix
- [x] 94.5 clicking Ember showed "This page couldn't load" (then a reload worked): find the
      cause (memory / GPU when switching designs) and fix
- [x] 94.6 Ember on a phone: the tucked chain still covers line ends at several scroll steps
      (QA 1de7b4e7: steps 4, 5, 7, 8, 10, 11, 14) — must never sit on words

The Active Theory feel (default design, desktop AND phone)
- [x] 94.7 the cards REVOLVE around the divider: small, coming round from behind, growing to
      face you, then on round behind as the next comes — clearly, not a slight tilt
- [x] 94.8 the divider becomes a crystal "skeleton" spine in the centre of the cards; on a
      phone it is centred behind the cards (not in the left margin)
- [x] 94.9 cards look like water crystal: glass with liquid/watercolour moving inside, an

- [x] 94.11 Ember: its star field zooms in only (it currently contracts back — "zoom out

- [x] 94.12 the cursor: things near it move, like a stone thrown in water, smoothly — replace

- [x] 94.13 the finale chain in the crystal/water/liquid style instead of the plain chain
- [x] 94.15 the fine dust of Ember's scene in the default design's background too, quiet —

- [x] 94.14 everything synced to the public repo after each deploy (his standing rule)

He confirmed on 22 Nov, after 0bce68dd: "lag flickering gone… thanks" (94.1, on his machine),

      a card's, the page background's. That is a WebGL canvas whose context was LOST: a
      phone allows only so many live WebGL contexts and kills the oldest when a page asks
      for more (the liquid menu added one more). Fix the cause (fewer contexts on a phone)
      and the symptom (a lost canvas hides itself instead of showing a white board), and
      give the page a dark base so nothing missing can ever show white

      into the water — fill the last emptiness; and the chain must reach the surface on

- THE MOBILE PROFILE (393x852 at DPR 2.75, CPU 4× and 1×, a CDP trace): the page was
  saturated before any scroll — 1 frame a second at 4×. Cause #1, mine: the chips' painted
  rim was a 51 KB data URL held in a CSS custom property; 20 chips restyled every frame
  through it — style recalc 53 s of 94 s of main-thread time, ~60 ms per chip, and 1418 of
  1859 paints were the chips. Cause #2: WebGL contexts made and dropped while scrolling
  (≈9 a pass, 1–4.5 s freezes at 4×, again on every pass back). Then the always-on canvas
  loops, and small per-frame repaints (the name's sheen).
